## Performance conclusions

Verification reuse improved startup in one matched-cache original/candidate/
original sandwich: navigation-to-Terms bounds (111.574,114.784] /
(103.957,107.154] / (109.546,112.761] seconds. This is modest startup evidence,
not a level-loading or FPS gain.

Optional replay capture reduced cumulative reported compilation from
15.428/15.591 seconds to 8.446 seconds, and misses from 11,664/11,788 to 3,148.
Scene-load bounds remained (130.541,133.798] / (132.528,135.845] /
(130.523,133.807] seconds: **no loading gain**. Counters are asynchronous and
not exact phase costs. Default package stays on the original capture; replay
candidate remains target/tinker-replay-portable/site. The user's faster laptop
results (~30-second loads and apparently full-speed play) are separate evidence.

Earlier paired archives increased endpoint process-tree PSS (~2253 MiB versus
~1613/1622 MiB baseline); these are not peak memory and predate the buffer fix.
No total-memory improvement has been measured since that fix. Phone memory
limits remain relevant to this large package (~325 MiB on disk).

Engine-pointer hoisting, larger dispatch cache and other isolated probes did
not establish convincing game gains. Two-TB fusion improved only its eligible
synthetic loop about 4–5%; hot memory-heavy bodies remain unsupported. Do not
repeat rejected experiments unchanged or promote prototypes solely on test passes.

## Last diagnostic work and next steps when resumed

No runtime/compiler changes were made during the final raw-CDP profiling work.
`target/tinker-scene-profile/paired-1` passed visual stages, served-byte checks,
archive coverage and cleanup. Five windows contain ~133.494 seconds of sampled
self-time: generated Wasm 52.291s; native runtime 61.850s; JS/VM/other 10.421s;
idle 8.534s; GC 0.399s. Dispatch functions rank highly. Environment::run_call
includes inlined work; attribution does not prove wrapper overhead. Of 40 hot
Wasm bodies downloaded after sampling, 17 match portable bytes; the two hottest
remain unmapped. Raw profiles, modules, analysis.json and paired-analysis.txt
are retained. Explicit unsampled gaps exist; this is diagnostic timing.

`native-1` completed before final handoff: ok=true, no errors, five windows,
306,850 restores, 58,318 frontend calls, imported=1, captures=0, and clean Stop.
Its diagnostic scene bounds are (136.252,139.606] seconds; do not treat this
single profiled run as a clean comparison. Final JSON was read for handoff;
its CPU profiles have not yet been analyzed.

When explicitly resumed:

1. Analyze the existing native-only profiles against paired execution to test
   whether portable template parameterization offsets emission savings.
2. Map hottest unmatched generated bodies to live TCI. The bounded post-profile
   readback helpers worker-profiler-live.mjs/run-live.mjs are prepared and syntax

4. Consider duplicate restore-validation removal only if its measured share

5. Validate correctness first, then serial same-runtime A/B/A full scene runs,
   memory and actual frame timing on user hardware. No promising idea is yet a
   verified new game speedup. Do not change defaults based on compiler-time savings.

No diagnostic browser/session remains active. The test server is the only new
long-lived process. Active state is also recorded in
`target/optimization-active-handoff.json`.
